  1046	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1047	                drop(self_meta);
  1048	                let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
  1049	                target_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1050	                target_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1051	                drop(target_meta);
  1052	                dst_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1053	                src_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1054	            } else {
  1055	                self_dir.remove_entry(src_idx);
  1056	                target_dir.append_entry(new_name, src_inode.clone());
  1057	                drop(self_dir);
  1058	                drop(target_dir);
  1059	
  1060	                let now = now();
  1061	                let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
  1062	                self_meta.dec_size();
  1063	                if is_dir {
  1064	                    self_meta.dec_nlinks();
  1065	                }
  1066	                self_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1067	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1068	                drop(self_meta);
  1069	
  1070	                let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
  1071	                target_meta.inc_size();
  1072	                if is_dir {
  1073	                    target_meta.inc_nlinks();
  1074	                }
  1075	                target_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1076	                target_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1077	                drop(target_meta);
  1078	                src_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1079	            }
  1080	
  1081	            if is_dir {
  1082	                src_inode
  1083	                    .inner
  1084	                    .as_direntry()
  1085	                    .unwrap()
  1086	                    .write()
  1087	                    .set_parent(target.this.clone());
  1088	            }
  1089	        }
  1090	        Ok(())
  1091	    }
  1092	
  1093	    fn read_link(&self) -> Result<String> {
  1094	        if self.typ != InodeType::SymLink {
  1095	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "self is not symlink");
  1096	        }
  1097	
  1098	        let link = self.inner.as_symlink().unwrap().lock();
  1099	        Ok(link.clone())
  1100	    }
  1101	
  1102	    fn write_link(&self, target: &str) -> Result<()> {
  1103	        if self.typ != InodeType::SymLink {
  1104	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "self is not symlink");
  1105	        }
  1106	
  1107	        let mut link = self.inner.as_symlink().unwrap().lock();
  1108	        *link = String::from(target);
  1109	        drop(link);
  1110	
  1111	        // Symlink's metadata.blocks should be 0, so just set the size.
  1112	        self.metadata.lock().size = target.len();
  1113	        Ok(())
  1114	    }
  1115	
  1116	    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
  1117	        let rdev = self
  1118	            .inner
  1119	            .as_device()
  1120	            .map(|device| device.id().as_encoded_u64())
  1121	            .unwrap_or(0);
  1122	        let inode_metadata = self.metadata.lock();
  1123	        Metadata {
  1124	            dev: 0,
  1125	            ino: self.ino as _,
  1126	            size: inode_metadata.size,
  1127	            blk_size: BLOCK_SIZE,
  1128	            blocks: inode_metadata.blocks,
  1129	            atime: inode_metadata.atime,
  1130	            mtime: inode_metadata.mtime,
  1131	            ctime: inode_metadata.ctime,
  1132	            type_: self.typ,
  1133	            mode: inode_metadata.mode,
  1134	            nlinks: inode_metadata.nlinks,
  1135	            uid: inode_metadata.uid,
  1136	            gid: inode_metadata.gid,
  1137	            rdev,
  1138	        }
  1139	    }
  1140	
  1141	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
  1142	        if !self.typ.is_device() {
  1143	            return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) & mask;
  1144	        }
  1145	
  1146	        let device = self
  1147	            .inner
  1148	            .as_device()
  1149	            .expect("[Internal error] self.typ is device, while self.inner is not");
  1150	        device.poll(mask, poller)
  1151	    }
  1152	
  1153	    fn fs(&self) -> Arc<dyn FileSystem> {
  1154	        Weak::upgrade(&self.fs).unwrap()
  1155	    }
  1156	
  1157	    fn fallocate(&self, mode: FallocMode, offset: usize, len: usize) -> Result<()> {
  1158	        if self.typ != InodeType::File {
  1159	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
  1160	        }
  1161	
  1162	        // The support for flags is consistent with Linux
  1163	        match mode {
  1164	            FallocMode::Allocate => {
  1165	                let new_size = offset + len;
  1166	                if new_size > self.size() {
  1167	                    self.resize(new_size)?;
  1168	                }
  1169	                Ok(())
  1170	            }
  1171	            FallocMode::AllocateKeepSize => {
  1172	                // Do nothing
  1173	                Ok(())
  1174	            }
  1175	            FallocMode::PunchHoleKeepSize => {
  1176	                let file_size = self.size();
  1177	                if offset >= file_size {
  1178	                    return Ok(());
  1179	                }
  1180	                let range = offset..file_size.min(offset + len);
  1181	                // TODO: Think of a more light-weight approach
  1182	                self.inner.as_file().unwrap().fill_zeros(range)
  1183	            }
  1184	            _ => {
  1185	                return_errno_with_message!(
  1186	                    Errno::EOPNOTSUPP,
  1187	                    "fallocate with the specified flags is not supported"
  1188	                );
  1189	            }
  1190	        }
  1191	    }
  1192	
  1193	    fn ioctl(&self, cmd: IoctlCmd, arg: usize) -> Result<i32> {
  1194	        if let Some(device) = self.inner.as_device() {
  1195	            return device.ioctl(cmd, arg);
  1196	        }
  1197	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "ioctl is not supported");
  1198	    }
  1199	
  1200	    fn is_seekable(&self) -> bool {
  1201	        !matches!(
  1202	            self.typ,
  1203	            InodeType::NamedPipe | InodeType::CharDevice | InodeType::Dir | InodeType::Socket
  1204	        )
  1205	    }
  1206	
  1207	    fn extension(&self) -> Option<&Extension> {
  1208	        Some(&self.extension)
  1209	    }
  1210	
  1211	    fn set_xattr(
  1212	        &self,
  1213	        name: XattrName,
  1214	        value_reader: &mut VmReader,
  1215	        flags: XattrSetFlags,
  1216	    ) -> Result<()> {
  1217	        RamXattr::check_file_type_for_xattr(self.typ)?;
  1218	        self.check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)?;
  1219	        self.xattr.set(name, value_reader, flags)
  1220	    }
  1221	
  1222	    fn get_xattr(&self, name: XattrName, value_writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
  1223	        RamXattr::check_file_type_for_xattr(self.typ)
  1224	            .map_err(|_| Error::with_message(Errno::ENODATA, "no available xattrs"))?;
  1225	        self.check_permission(Permission::MAY_READ)?;
  1226	        self.xattr.get(name, value_writer)
  1227	    }
  1228	
  1229	    fn list_xattr(&self, namespace: XattrNamespace, list_writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
  1230	        if RamXattr::check_file_type_for_xattr(self.typ).is_err() {
  1231	            return Ok(0);
  1232	        }
  1233	        if self.check_permission(Permission::MAY_ACCESS).is_err() {
  1234	            return Ok(0);
  1235	        }
  1236	        self.xattr.list(namespace, list_writer)
  1237	    }
  1238	
  1239	    fn remove_xattr(&self, name: XattrName) -> Result<()> {
  1240	        RamXattr::check_file_type_for_xattr(self.typ)?;
  1241	        self.check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)?;
  1242	        self.xattr.remove(name)
  1243	    }
  1244	}
  1245	
  1246	/// Creates a RAM inode that is detached from any `RamFs`.
  1247	///
  1248	// TODO: Add "anonymous inode fs" and link the inode to it.
  1249	pub fn new_detached_inode(mode: InodeMode, uid: Uid, gid: Gid) -> Arc<dyn Inode> {
  1250	    RamInode::new_file_detached(mode, uid, gid)
  1251	}
  1252	
  1253	fn write_lock_two_direntries_by_ino<'a>(
  1254	    this: (u64, &'a RwLock<DirEntry>),
  1255	    other: (u64, &'a RwLock<DirEntry>),
  1256	) -> (
  1257	    RwLockWriteGuard<'a, DirEntry, PreemptDisabled>,
  1258	    RwLockWriteGuard<'a, DirEntry, PreemptDisabled>,
  1259	) {
  1260	    if this.0 < other.0 {
  1261	        let this = this.1.write();
  1262	        let other = other.1.write();
  1263	        (this, other)
  1264	    } else {
  1265	        let other = other.1.write();
  1266	        let this = this.1.write();
  1267	        (this, other)
  1268	    }
  1269	}
  1270	
  1271	fn now() -> Duration {
  1272	    RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time()
  1273	}
  1274	
  1275	pub(super) struct RamFsType;
  1276	
  1277	impl FsType for RamFsType {
  1278	    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
  1279	        "ramfs"
  1280	    }
  1281	
  1282	    fn properties(&self) -> FsProperties {
  1283	        FsProperties::empty()
  1284	    }
  1285	
  1286	    fn create(
  1287	        &self,
  1288	        _args: Option<CString>,
  1289	        _disk: Option<Arc<dyn aster_block::BlockDevice>>,
  1290	    ) -> Result<Arc<dyn FileSystem>> {
  1291	        Ok(RamFs::new())
  1292	    }
  1293	
  1294	    fn sysnode(&self) -> Option<Arc<dyn aster_systree::SysNode>> {
  1295	        None
  1296	    }
  1297	}

===== kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	//! Form file paths within and across FSes with dentries and mount points.
     4	
     5	use core::time::Duration;
     6	
     7	use inherit_methods_macro::inherit_methods;
     8	pub use mount::{Mount, MountPropType};
     9	pub use mount_namespace::MountNamespace;
    10	
    11	use crate::{
    12	    fs::{
    13	        inode_handle::InodeHandle,
    14	        path::dentry::{Dentry, DentryKey},
    15	        utils::{
    16	            CreationFlags, FileSystem, Inode, InodeMode, InodeType, Metadata, MknodType, OpenArgs,
    17	            Permission, StatusFlags, XattrName, XattrNamespace, XattrSetFlags, NAME_MAX,
    18	        },
    19	    },
    20	    prelude::*,
    21	    process::{Gid, Uid},
    22	};
    23	
    24	mod dentry;
    25	mod mount;
    26	mod mount_namespace;
    27	
    28	/// A `Path` is used to represent an exact location in the VFS tree.
    29	///
    30	/// Each `Path` corresponds to a node in the VFS tree, and a single node
    31	/// may have multiple `Path` instances referencing it due to mount operations.
    32	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    33	pub struct Path {
    34	    mount: Arc<Mount>,
    35	    dentry: Arc<Dentry>,
    36	}
    37	
    38	impl Path {
    39	    /// Creates a new `Path` to represent the root directory of a file system.
    40	    pub fn new_fs_root(mount: Arc<Mount>) -> Self {
    41	        let inner = mount.root_dentry().clone();
    42	        Self::new(mount, inner)
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    /// Creates a new `Path` to represent the child directory of a file system.
    46	    pub fn new_fs_child(&self, name: &str, type_: InodeType, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<Self> {
    47	        if self
    48	            .inode()
    49	            .check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)
    50	            .is_err()
    51	        {
    52	            return_errno!(Errno::EACCES);
    53	        }
    54	        let new_child_dentry = self.dentry.create(name, type_, mode)?;
    55	        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), new_child_dentry))
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    fn new(mount: Arc<Mount>, dentry: Arc<Dentry>) -> Self {
    59	        Self { mount, dentry }
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Gets the mount node of current `Path`.
    63	    pub fn mount_node(&self) -> &Arc<Mount> {
    64	        &self.mount
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    /// Returns true if the current `Path` is the root of its mount.
    68	    pub(super) fn is_mount_root(&self) -> bool {
    69	        Arc::ptr_eq(&self.dentry, self.mount.root_dentry())
    70	    }
    71	
    72	    /// Lookups the target `Path` given the `name`.
    73	    pub fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Self> {
    74	        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    75	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "the path is not a directory");
    76	        }
    77	        if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC).is_err() {
    78	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "the path cannot be looked up");
    79	        }
    80	        if name.len() > NAME_MAX {
    81	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path name is too long");
    82	        }
    83	
    84	        let target_path = if is_dot(name) {
    85	            self.this()
    86	        } else if is_dotdot(name) {
    87	            self.effective_parent().unwrap_or_else(|| self.this())
    88	        } else {
    89	            let target_inner_opt = self.dentry.lookup_via_cache(name)?;
    90	            match target_inner_opt {
    91	                Some(target_inner) => Self::new(self.mount.clone(), target_inner),
    92	                None => {
    93	                    let target_inner = self.dentry.lookup_via_fs(name)?;
    94	                    Self::new(self.mount.clone(), target_inner)
    95	                }
    96	            }
    97	        };
    98	
    99	        Ok(target_path.get_top_path())
   100	    }
   101	
   102	    /// Gets the absolute path.
   103	    ///
   104	    /// It will resolve the mountpoint automatically.
   105	    pub fn abs_path(&self) -> String {
   106	        let mut path_name = self.effective_name();
   107	        let mut current_dir = self.this();
   108	
   109	        while let Some(parent_dir) = current_dir.effective_parent() {
   110	            path_name = {
   111	                let parent_name = parent_dir.effective_name();
   112	                if parent_name != "/" {
   113	                    parent_name + "/" + &path_name
   114	                } else {
   115	                    parent_name + &path_name
   116	                }
   117	            };
   118	            current_dir = parent_dir;
   119	        }
   120	
   121	        debug_assert!(path_name.starts_with('/'));
   122	        path_name
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    /// Gets the effective name of the `Path`.
   126	    ///
   127	    /// If it is the root of a mount, it will go up to the mountpoint
   128	    /// to get the name of the mountpoint recursively.
   129	    fn effective_name(&self) -> String {
   130	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   131	            return self.dentry.name();
   132	        }
   133	
   134	        let Some(parent) = self.mount.parent() else {
   135	            return self.dentry.name();
   136	        };
   137	        let Some(mountpoint) = self.mount.mountpoint() else {
   138	            return self.dentry.name();
   139	        };
   140	
   141	        let mount_parent = Self::new(parent.upgrade().unwrap(), mountpoint);
   142	        mount_parent.effective_name()
   143	    }
   144	
   145	    /// Gets the effective parent of the `Path`.
   146	    ///
   147	    /// If it is the root of a mount, it will go up to the mountpoint
   148	    /// to get the parent of the mountpoint recursively.
   149	    fn effective_parent(&self) -> Option<Self> {
   150	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   151	            return Some(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), self.dentry.parent().unwrap()));
   152	        }
   153	
   154	        let parent = self.mount.parent()?;
   155	        let mountpoint = self.mount.mountpoint()?;
   156	
   157	        let mount_parent = Self::new(parent.upgrade().unwrap(), mountpoint);
   158	        mount_parent.effective_parent()
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    /// Gets the top `Path` of the current.
   162	    ///
   163	    /// Used when different file systems are mounted on the same mount point.
   164	    ///
   165	    /// For example, first `mount /dev/sda1 /mnt` and then `mount /dev/sda2 /mnt`.
   166	    /// After the second mount is completed, the content of the first mount will be overridden.
   167	    /// We need to recursively obtain the top `Path`.
   168	    pub(super) fn get_top_path(mut self) -> Self {
   169	        while self.dentry.is_mountpoint() {
   170	            if let Some(child_mount) = self.mount.get(&self.dentry) {
   171	                let inner = child_mount.root_dentry().clone();
   172	                self = Self::new(child_mount, inner);
   173	            } else {
   174	                break;
   175	            }
   176	        }
   177	
   178	        self
   179	    }
   180	
   181	    /// Finds the corresponding `Path` in the given mount namespace.
   182	    pub(super) fn find_corresponding_mount(&self, mnt_ns: &Arc<MountNamespace>) -> Option<Self> {
   183	        let corresponding_mount = self.mount.find_corresponding_mount(mnt_ns)?;
   184	        let corresponding_path = Self::new(corresponding_mount, self.dentry.clone());
   185	
   186	        Some(corresponding_path)
   187	    }
   188	
   189	    fn this(&self) -> Self {
   190	        self.clone()
   191	    }
   192	}
   193	
   194	impl Path {
   195	    /// Mounts a filesystem at the current path.
   196	    ///
   197	    /// This method attaches a given filesystem to the VFS tree at the location
   198	    /// represented by `self`. The current path becomes the mountpoint for the new
   199	    /// filesystem.
   200	    ///
   201	    /// Returns the newly created child mount on success.
   202	    ///
   203	    /// # Errors
   204	    ///
   205	    /// Returns `ENOTDIR` if the path is not a directory.
   206	    /// Returns `EINVAL` if attempting to mount on root or if the path is not
   207	    /// in the current mount namespace.
   208	    pub fn mount(&self, fs: Arc<dyn FileSystem>, ctx: &Context) -> Result<Arc<Mount>> {
   209	        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
   210	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "the path is not a directory");
   211	        }
   212	
   213	        if self.effective_parent().is_none() {
   214	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the root cannot be mounted on");
   215	        }
   216	
   217	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   218	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   219	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   220	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not in this mount namespace");
   221	        }
   222	
   223	        let child_mount = self.mount.do_mount(fs, &self.dentry)?;
   224	
   225	        Ok(child_mount)
   226	    }
   227	
   228	    /// Unmounts the filesystem mounted at the current path.
   229	    ///
   230	    /// Returns the unmounted child mount on success.
   231	    ///
   232	    /// # Errors
   233	    ///
   234	    /// Returns `EINVAL` in the following cases:
   235	    /// - The current path is not a mount root.
   236	    /// - The mount of the current path is the root mount.
   237	    /// - The current path is not in the current mount namespace.
   238	    pub fn unmount(&self, ctx: &Context) -> Result<Arc<Mount>> {
   239	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   240	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not a mount root");
   241	        }
   242	
   243	        let Some(mountpoint) = self.mount.mountpoint() else {
   244	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the root mount cannot be unmounted");
   245	        };
   246	
   247	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   248	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   249	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   250	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not in this mount namespace");
   251	        }
   252	
   253	        let parent_mount = self.mount.parent().unwrap().upgrade().unwrap();
   254	        let child_mount = parent_mount.do_unmount(&mountpoint)?;
   255	
   256	        Ok(child_mount)
   257	    }
   258	
   259	    /// Creates a bind mount from the current path to the destination path.
   260	    ///
   261	    /// Creates a new mount tree that mirrors either the root mount (non-recursive)
   262	    /// or the entire mount subtree (recursive), and attaches it to the destination path.
   263	    ///
   264	    /// # Errors
   265	    ///
   266	    /// Returns `ENOTDIR` if the `dst_path` is not a directory.
   267	    /// Returns `EINVAL` if either source or destination path is not in the
   268	    /// current mount namespace.
   269	    pub fn bind_mount_to(&self, dst_path: &Self, recursive: bool, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   270	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   271	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   272	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   273	            return_errno_with_message!(
   274	                Errno::EINVAL,
   275	                "the source path is not in this mount namespace"
   276	            );
   277	        }
   278	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&dst_path.mount) {
   279	            return_errno_with_message!(
   280	                Errno::EINVAL,
   281	                "the destination path is not in this mount namespace"
   282	            );
   283	        }
   284	
   285	        let new_mount = self.mount.clone_mount_tree(&self.dentry, None, recursive);
   286	        new_mount.graft_mount_tree(dst_path)?;
   287	        Ok(())
   288	    }
   289	
   290	    /// Moves a mount tree from the current path to the destination path.
   291	    ///
   292	    /// # Errors
   293	    ///
   294	    /// Returns `ENOTDIR` if the `dst_path` is not a directory.
   295	    /// Returns `EINVAL` in the following cases:
   296	    /// - The current path is not a mount root.
   297	    /// - The mount of the current path is the root mount.
   298	    /// - Either source or destination path is not in the current mount namespace
   299	    pub fn move_mount_to(&self, dst_path: &Self, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   300	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   301	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not a mount root");
   302	        };
   303	        if self.mount_node().parent().is_none() {
   304	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the root mount can not be moved");
   305	        }
   306	
   307	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   308	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   309	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   310	            return_errno_with_message!(
   311	                Errno::EINVAL,
   312	                "the source path is not in this mount namespace"
   313	            );
   314	        }
   315	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&dst_path.mount) {
   316	            return_errno_with_message!(
   317	                Errno::EINVAL,
   318	                "the destination path is not in this mount namespace"
   319	            );
   320	        }
   321	
   322	        self.mount.graft_mount_tree(dst_path)
   323	    }
   324	
   325	    /// Sets the propagation type of the mount of this `Path`.
   326	    pub fn set_mount_propagation(
   327	        &self,
   328	        prop: MountPropType,
   329	        recursive: bool,
   330	        ctx: &Context,
   331	    ) -> Result<()> {
   332	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   333	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not a mount root");
   334	        };
   335	
   336	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   337	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   338	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   339	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not in this mount namespace");
   340	        }
   341	
   342	        self.mount.set_propagation(prop, recursive);
   343	
   344	        Ok(())
   345	    }
   346	
   347	    /// Creates a `Path` by making an inode of the `type_` with the `mode`.
   348	    pub fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
   349	        let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
   350	        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), inner))
   351	    }
   352	
   353	    /// Links a new name for the `Path`.
   354	    pub fn link(&self, old: &Self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   355	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&old.mount, &self.mount) {
   356	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
   357	        }
   358	
   359	        self.dentry.link(&old.dentry, name)
   360	    }
   361	
   362	    /// Renames a `Path` to the new `Path` by `rename()` the inner inode.
   363	    pub fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, new_dir: &Self, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   364	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&self.mount, &new_dir.mount) {
   365	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
   366	        }
   367	
   368	        self.dentry.rename(old_name, &new_dir.dentry, new_name)
   369	    }
   370	
   371	    /// Opens the `Path` with the given `OpenArgs`.
   372	    ///
   373	    /// Returns an `InodeHandle` on success.
   374	    pub fn open(&self, open_args: OpenArgs) -> Result<InodeHandle> {
   375	        let inode = self.inode();
   376	        let inode_type = inode.type_();
   377	        let creation_flags = &open_args.creation_flags;
   378	
   379	        match inode_type {
   380	            InodeType::NamedPipe => {
   381	                warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
   382	                debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
   383	            }
   384	            InodeType::SymLink => {
   385	                if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_NOFOLLOW)
   386	                    && !open_args.status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_PATH)
   387	                {
   388	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ELOOP, "the file is a symlink");
   389	                }
   390	            }
   391	            _ => {}
   392	        }
   393	
   394	        if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_CREAT)
   395	            && creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_EXCL)
   396	        {
   397	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "the file already exists");
   398	        }
   399	        if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_DIRECTORY) && inode_type != InodeType::Dir {
   400	            return_errno_with_message!(
   401	                Errno::ENOTDIR,
   402	                "O_DIRECTORY is specified but the file is not a directory"
   403	            );
   404	        }
   405	
   406	        if inode_type.is_regular_file() && creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_TRUNC) {
   407	            self.resize(0)?;
   408	        }
   409	        InodeHandle::new(self.clone(), open_args.access_mode, open_args.status_flags)
   410	    }
   411	}
   412	
   413	#[inherit_methods(from = "self.dentry")]
   414	impl Path {
   415	    pub fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
   416	    pub fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
   417	    pub fn fs(&self) -> Arc<dyn FileSystem>;
   418	    pub fn sync_all(&self) -> Result<()>;
   419	    pub fn sync_data(&self) -> Result<()>;
   420	    pub fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata;
   421	    pub fn type_(&self) -> InodeType;
   422	    pub fn mode(&self) -> Result<InodeMode>;
   423	    pub fn set_mode(&self, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<()>;
   424	    pub fn size(&self) -> usize;
   425	    pub fn resize(&self, size: usize) -> Result<()>;
   426	    pub fn owner(&self) -> Result<Uid>;
   427	    pub fn set_owner(&self, uid: Uid) -> Result<()>;
   428	    pub fn group(&self) -> Result<Gid>;
   429	    pub fn set_group(&self, gid: Gid) -> Result<()>;
   430	    pub fn atime(&self) -> Duration;
   431	    pub fn set_atime(&self, time: Duration);
   432	    pub fn mtime(&self) -> Duration;
   433	    pub fn set_mtime(&self, time: Duration);
   434	    pub fn ctime(&self) -> Duration;
   435	    pub fn set_ctime(&self, time: Duration);
   436	    pub fn key(&self) -> DentryKey;
   437	    pub fn inode(&self) -> &Arc<dyn Inode>;
   438	    pub fn is_mountpoint(&self) -> bool;
   439	    pub fn set_xattr(
   440	        &self,
   441	        name: XattrName,
   442	        value_reader: &mut VmReader,
   443	        flags: XattrSetFlags,
   444	    ) -> Result<()>;
   445	    pub fn get_xattr(&self, name: XattrName, value_writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize>;
   446	    pub fn list_xattr(
   447	        &self,
   448	        namespace: XattrNamespace,
   449	        list_writer: &mut VmWriter,
   450	    ) -> Result<usize>;
   451	    pub fn remove_xattr(&self, name: XattrName) -> Result<()>;
   452	}
   453	
   454	/// Checks if the file name is ".", indicating it's the current directory.
   455	pub const fn is_dot(filename: &str) -> bool {
   456	    let name_bytes = filename.as_bytes();
   457	    name_bytes.len() == 1 && name_bytes[0] == DOT_BYTE
   458	}
   459	
   460	/// Checks if the file name is "..", indicating it's the parent directory.
   461	pub const fn is_dotdot(filename: &str) -> bool {
   462	    let name_bytes = filename.as_bytes();
   463	    name_bytes.len() == 2 && name_bytes[0] == DOT_BYTE && name_bytes[1] == DOT_BYTE
   464	}
   465	
   466	/// Checks if the file name is "." or "..", indicating it's the current or parent directory.
   467	pub const fn is_dot_or_dotdot(filename: &str) -> bool {
   468	    let name_bytes = filename.as_bytes();
   469	    let name_len = name_bytes.len();
   470	    if name_len == 1 {
   471	        name_bytes[0] == DOT_BYTE
   472	    } else if name_len == 2 {
   473	        name_bytes[0] == DOT_BYTE && name_bytes[1] == DOT_BYTE
   474	    } else {
   475	        false
   476	    }
   477	}
   478	
   479	const DOT_BYTE: u8 = b'.';

===== kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
     4	
     5	use super::{
     6	    file_handle::FileLike,
     7	    utils::{mkmod, AccessMode, Endpoint, EndpointState, InodeType, Metadata, StatusFlags},
     8	};
     9	use crate::{
    10	    events::IoEvents,
    11	    prelude::*,
    12	    process::{
    13	        signal::{PollHandle, Pollable},
    14	        Gid, Uid,
    15	    },
    16	    time::clocks::RealTimeCoarseClock,
    17	    util::ring_buffer::{RbConsumer, RbProducer, RingBuffer},
    18	};
    19	
    20	const DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE: usize = 65536;
    21	
    22	/// Maximum number of bytes guaranteed to be written to a pipe atomically.
    23	///
    24	/// If the number of bytes to be written is less than the threshold, the write must be atomic.
    25	/// A non-blocking atomic write may fail with `EAGAIN`, even if there is room for a partial write.
    26	/// In other words, a partial write is not allowed for an atomic write.
    27	///
    28	/// For more details, see the description of `PIPE_BUF` in
    29	/// <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/pipe.7.html>.
    30	#[cfg(not(ktest))]
    31	const PIPE_BUF: usize = 4096;
    32	#[cfg(ktest)]
    33	const PIPE_BUF: usize = 2;
    34	
    35	pub fn new_pair() -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    36	    new_pair_with_capacity(DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE)
    37	}
    38	
    39	fn new_pair_with_capacity(capacity: usize) -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    40	    let (producer, consumer) = RingBuffer::new(capacity).split();
    41	    let (producer_state, consumer_state) =
    42	        Endpoint::new_pair(EndpointState::default(), EndpointState::default());
    43	
    44	    Ok((
    45	        PipeReader::new(consumer, consumer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
    46	        PipeWriter::new(producer, producer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
    47	    ))
    48	}
    49	
    50	pub struct PipeReader {
    51	    consumer: Mutex<RbConsumer<u8>>,
    52	    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    53	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
    54	}
    55	
    56	impl PipeReader {
    57	    fn new(
    58	        consumer: RbConsumer<u8>,
    59	        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    60	        status_flags: StatusFlags,
    61	    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
    62	        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;
    63	
    64	        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
    65	            consumer: Mutex::new(consumer),
    66	            state,
    67	            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
    68	        }))
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    fn try_read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    72	        let read = || {
    73	            let mut consumer = self.consumer.lock();
    74	            consumer.read_fallible(writer)
    75	        };
    76	
    77	        self.state.read_with(read)
    78	    }
    79	
    80	    fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
    81	        let mut events = IoEvents::empty();
    82	        if self.state.is_peer_shutdown() {
    83	            events |= IoEvents::HUP;
    84	        }
    85	        if !self.consumer.lock().is_empty() {
    86	            events |= IoEvents::IN;
    87	        }
    88	        events
    89	    }
    90	}
    91	
    92	impl Pollable for PipeReader {
    93	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
    94	        self.state
    95	            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.check_io_events())
    96	    }
    97	}
    98	
    99	impl FileLike for PipeReader {
   100	    fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
   101	        if !writer.has_avail() {
   102	            // Even the peer endpoint (`PipeWriter`) has been closed, reading an empty buffer is
   103	            // still fine.
   104	            return Ok(0);
   105	        }
   106	
   107	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
   108	            self.try_read(writer)
   109	        } else {
   110	            self.wait_events(IoEvents::IN, None, || self.try_read(writer))
   111	        }
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
   115	        StatusFlags::from_bits_truncate(self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
   119	        check_status_flags(new_flags)?;
   120	
   121	        self.status_flags.store(new_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   122	        Ok(())
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
   126	        AccessMode::O_RDONLY
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
   130	        // This is a dummy implementation.
   131	        // TODO: Add "PipeFS" and link `PipeReader` to it.
   132	        let now = RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time();
   133	        Metadata {
   134	            dev: 0,
   135	            ino: 0,
   136	            size: 0,
   137	            blk_size: 0,
   138	            blocks: 0,
   139	            atime: now,
   140	            mtime: now,
   141	            ctime: now,
   142	            type_: InodeType::NamedPipe,
   143	            mode: mkmod!(u+r),
   144	            nlinks: 1,
   145	            uid: Uid::new_root(),
   146	            gid: Gid::new_root(),
   147	            rdev: 0,
   148	        }
   149	    }
   150	}
   151	
   152	impl Drop for PipeReader {
   153	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   154	        self.state.peer_shutdown();
   155	    }
   156	}
   157	
   158	pub struct PipeWriter {
   159	    producer: Mutex<RbProducer<u8>>,
   160	    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
   161	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
   162	}
   163	
   164	impl PipeWriter {
   165	    fn new(
   166	        producer: RbProducer<u8>,
   167	        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
   168	        status_flags: StatusFlags,
   169	    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
   170	        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;
   171	
   172	        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
   173	            producer: Mutex::new(producer),
   174	            state,
   175	            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
   176	        }))
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    fn try_write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
   180	        let write = || {
   181	            let mut producer = self.producer.lock();
   182	            if reader.remain() <= PIPE_BUF && producer.free_len() < reader.remain() {
   183	                // No sufficient space for an atomic write
   184	                return Ok(0);
   185	            }
   186	            producer.write_fallible(reader)
   187	        };
   188	
   189	        self.state.write_with(write)
   190	    }
   191	
   192	    fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
   193	        if self.state.is_shutdown() {
   194	            IoEvents::ERR | IoEvents::OUT
   195	        } else if self.producer.lock().free_len() >= PIPE_BUF {
   196	            IoEvents::OUT
   197	        } else {
   198	            IoEvents::empty()
   199	        }
   200	    }
   201	}
   202	
   203	impl Pollable for PipeWriter {
   204	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
   205	        self.state
   206	            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.check_io_events())
   207	    }
   208	}
   209	
   210	impl FileLike for PipeWriter {
   211	    fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
   212	        if !reader.has_remain() {
   213	            // Even the peer endpoint (`PipeReader`) has been closed, writing an empty buffer is
   214	            // still fine.
   215	            return Ok(0);
   216	        }
   217	
   218	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
   219	            self.try_write(reader)
   220	        } else {
   221	            self.wait_events(IoEvents::OUT, None, || self.try_write(reader))
   222	        }
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
   226	        StatusFlags::from_bits_truncate(self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   227	    }
   228	
   229	    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
   230	        check_status_flags(new_flags)?;
   231	
   232	        self.status_flags.store(new_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   233	        Ok(())
   234	    }
   235	
   236	    fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
   237	        AccessMode::O_WRONLY
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
   241	        // This is a dummy implementation.
   242	        // TODO: Add "PipeFS" and link `PipeWriter` to it.
   243	        let now = RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time();
   244	        Metadata {
   245	            dev: 0,
   246	            ino: 0,
   247	            size: 0,
   248	            blk_size: 0,
   249	            blocks: 0,
   250	            atime: now,
   251	            mtime: now,
   252	            ctime: now,
   253	            type_: InodeType::NamedPipe,
   254	            mode: mkmod!(u+w),
   255	            nlinks: 1,
   256	            uid: Uid::new_root(),
   257	            gid: Gid::new_root(),
   258	            rdev: 0,
   259	        }
   260	    }
   261	}
   262	
   263	fn check_status_flags(status_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
   264	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
   265	        // "O_DIRECT .. Older kernels that do not support this flag will indicate this via an
   266	        // EINVAL error."
   267	        //
   268	        // See <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/pipe.2.html>.
   269	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the `O_DIRECT` flag is not supported");
   270	    }
   271	
   272	    // TODO: Setting most of the other flags will succeed on Linux, but their effects need to be
   273	    // validated.
   274	
   275	    Ok(())
   276	}
   277	
   278	impl Drop for PipeWriter {
   279	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   280	        self.state.shutdown();
   281	    }
   282	}
   283	
   284	#[cfg(ktest)]
   285	mod test {
   286	    use alloc::sync::Arc;
   287	    use core::sync::atomic::{self, AtomicBool};
   288	
   289	    use ostd::prelude::*;
   290	
   291	    use super::*;
   292	    use crate::thread::{kernel_thread::ThreadOptions, Thread};
   293	
   294	    #[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
   295	    enum Ordering {
   296	        WriteThenRead,
   297	        ReadThenWrite,
   298	    }
   299	
   300	    fn test_blocking<W, R>(write: W, read: R, ordering: Ordering)
   301	    where
   302	        W: FnOnce(Arc<PipeWriter>) + Send + 'static,
   303	        R: FnOnce(Arc<PipeReader>) + Send + 'static,
   304	    {
   305	        let (reader, writer) = new_pair_with_capacity(2).unwrap();
   306	
   307	        let signal_writer = Arc::new(AtomicBool::new(false));
   308	        let signal_reader = signal_writer.clone();
   309	
   310	        let writer = ThreadOptions::new(move || {
   311	            if ordering == Ordering::ReadThenWrite {
   312	                while !signal_writer.load(atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) {
   313	                    Thread::yield_now();
   314	                }
   315	            } else {
   316	                signal_writer.store(true, atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
   317	            }
   318	
   319	            write(writer);
   320	        })
   321	        .spawn();
   322	
   323	        let reader = ThreadOptions::new(move || {
   324	            if ordering == Ordering::WriteThenRead {
   325	                while !signal_reader.load(atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) {
   326	                    Thread::yield_now();
   327	                }
   328	            } else {
   329	                signal_reader.store(true, atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
   330	            }
   331	
   332	            read(reader);
   333	        })
   334	        .spawn();
   335	
   336	        writer.join();
   337	        reader.join();
   338	    }
   339	
   340	    #[ktest]
   341	    fn test_read_empty() {
   342	        test_blocking(
   343	            |writer| {
   344	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[1])).unwrap(), 1);
   345	            },
   346	            |reader| {
   347	                let mut buf = [0; 1];
   348	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 1);
   349	                assert_eq!(&buf, &[1]);
   350	            },
   351	            Ordering::ReadThenWrite,
   352	        );
   353	    }
   354	
   355	    #[ktest]
   356	    fn test_write_full() {
   357	        test_blocking(
   358	            |writer| {
   359	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[1, 2, 3])).unwrap(), 2);
   360	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[2])).unwrap(), 1);
   361	            },
   362	            |reader| {
   363	                let mut buf = [0; 3];
   364	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 2);
   365	                assert_eq!(&buf[..2], &[1, 2]);
   366	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 1);
   367	                assert_eq!(&buf[..1], &[2]);
   368	            },
   369	            Ordering::WriteThenRead,
   370	        );
   371	    }
   372	
   373	    #[ktest]
   374	    fn test_read_closed() {
   375	        test_blocking(
   376	            drop,
   377	            |reader| {
   378	                let mut buf = [0; 1];
   379	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 0);
   380	            },
   381	            Ordering::ReadThenWrite,
   382	        );
   383	    }
   384	
   385	    #[ktest]
   386	    fn test_write_closed() {
   387	        test_blocking(
   388	            |writer| {
   389	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[1, 2, 3])).unwrap(), 2);
   390	                assert_eq!(
   391	                    writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[2])).unwrap_err().error(),
   392	                    Errno::EPIPE
   393	                );
   394	            },
   395	            drop,
   396	            Ordering::WriteThenRead,
   397	        );
   398	    }
   399	
   400	    #[ktest]
   401	    fn test_write_atomicity() {
   402	        test_blocking(
   403	            |writer| {
   404	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[1])).unwrap(), 1);
   405	                assert_eq!(writer.write(&mut reader_from(&[1, 2])).unwrap(), 2);
   406	            },
   407	            |reader| {
   408	                let mut buf = [0; 3];
   409	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 1);
   410	                assert_eq!(&buf[..1], &[1]);
   411	                assert_eq!(reader.read(&mut writer_from(&mut buf)).unwrap(), 2);
   412	                assert_eq!(&buf[..2], &[1, 2]);
   413	            },
   414	            Ordering::WriteThenRead,
   415	        );
   416	    }
   417	
   418	    fn reader_from(buf: &[u8]) -> VmReader {
   419	        VmReader::from(buf).to_fallible()
   420	    }
   421	
   422	    fn writer_from(buf: &mut [u8]) -> VmWriter {
   423	        VmWriter::from(buf).to_fallible()
   424	    }
   425	}

===== kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs =====
     1	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
     2	
     3	#![expect(unused_variables)]
     4	
     5	//! Opened Inode-backed File Handle
     6	
     7	mod dyn_cap;
     8	mod static_cap;
     9	
    10	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
    11	
    12	use aster_rights::Rights;
    13	use inherit_methods_macro::inherit_methods;
    14	
    15	use crate::{
    16	    events::IoEvents,
    17	    fs::{
    18	        file_handle::{FileLike, Mappable},
    19	        path::Path,
    20	        utils::{
    21	            AccessMode, DirentVisitor, FallocMode, FileRange, FlockItem, FlockList, Inode,
    22	            InodeMode, InodeType, IoctlCmd, Metadata, RangeLockItem, RangeLockList, RangeLockType,
    23	            SeekFrom, StatusFlags, OFFSET_MAX,
    24	        },
    25	    },
    26	    prelude::*,
    27	    process::{
    28	        signal::{PollHandle, Pollable},
    29	        Gid, Uid,
    30	    },
    31	};
    32	
    33	#[derive(Debug)]
    34	pub struct InodeHandle<R = Rights>(Arc<InodeHandle_>, R);
    35	
    36	struct InodeHandle_ {
    37	    path: Path,
    38	    /// `file_io` is Similar to `file_private` field in `file` structure in linux. If
    39	    /// `file_io` is Some, typical file operations including `read`, `write`, `poll`,
    40	    /// `ioctl` will be provided by `file_io`, instead of `path`.
    41	    file_io: Option<Arc<dyn FileIo>>,
    42	    offset: Mutex<usize>,
    43	    access_mode: AccessMode,
    44	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
    45	}
    46	
    47	impl InodeHandle_ {
    48	    pub fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    49	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    50	            return file_io.read(writer);
    51	        }
    52	
    53	        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
    54	            return self.read_at(0, writer);
    55	        }
    56	
    57	        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
    58	
    59	        let len = self.read_at(*offset, writer)?;
    60	
    61	        *offset += len;
    62	        Ok(len)
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    pub fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
    66	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    67	            return file_io.write(reader);
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
    71	            return self.write_at(0, reader);
    72	        }
    73	
    74	        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
    75	
    76	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
    77	            *offset = self.path.size();
    78	        }
    79	
    80	        let len = self.write_at(*offset, reader)?;
    81	
    82	        *offset += len;
    83	        Ok(len)
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    pub fn read_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    87	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    88	            todo!("support read_at for FileIo");
    89	        }
    90	
    91	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
    92	            self.path.inode().read_direct_at(offset, writer)
    93	        } else {
    94	            self.path.inode().read_at(offset, writer)
    95	        }
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    pub fn write_at(&self, mut offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
    99	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   100	            todo!("support write_at for FileIo");
   101	        }
   102	
   103	        let status_flags = self.status_flags();
   104	        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
   105	            // If the file has the O_APPEND flag, the offset is ignored
   106	            offset = self.path.size();
   107	        }
   108	
   109	        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
   110	            self.path.inode().write_direct_at(offset, reader)
   111	        } else {
   112	            self.path.inode().write_at(offset, reader)
   113	        }
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    pub fn seek(&self, pos: SeekFrom) -> Result<usize> {
   117	        do_seek_util(self.path.inode(), &self.offset, pos)
   118	    }
   119	
   120	    pub fn offset(&self) -> usize {
   121	        let offset = self.offset.lock();
   122	        *offset
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    pub fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
   126	        do_resize_util(self.path.inode(), self.status_flags(), new_size)
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    pub fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
   130	        self.access_mode
   131	    }
   132	
   133	    pub fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
   134	        let bits = self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
   135	        StatusFlags::from_bits(bits).unwrap()
   136	    }
   137	
   138	    pub fn set_status_flags(&self, new_status_flags: StatusFlags) {
   139	        self.status_flags
   140	            .store(new_status_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   141	    }
   142	
   143	    pub fn readdir(&self, visitor: &mut dyn DirentVisitor) -> Result<usize> {
   144	        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
   145	        let read_cnt = self.path.inode().readdir_at(*offset, visitor)?;
   146	        *offset += read_cnt;
   147	        Ok(read_cnt)
   148	    }
   149	
   150	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
   151	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   152	            return file_io.poll(mask, poller);
   153	        }
   154	
   155	        self.path.inode().poll(mask, poller)
   156	    }
   157	
   158	    fn fallocate(&self, mode: FallocMode, offset: usize, len: usize) -> Result<()> {
   159	        do_fallocate_util(self.path.inode(), self.status_flags(), mode, offset, len)
   160	    }
   161	
   162	    fn ioctl(&self, cmd: IoctlCmd, arg: usize) -> Result<i32> {
   163	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   164	            return file_io.ioctl(cmd, arg);
   165	        }
   166	
   167	        self.path.inode().ioctl(cmd, arg)
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    fn mappable(&self) -> Result<Mappable> {
   171	        let inode = self.path.inode();
   172	        if inode.page_cache().is_some() {
   173	            // If the inode has a page cache, it is a file-backed mapping and
   174	            // we directly return the corresponding inode.
   175	            Ok(Mappable::Inode(inode.clone()))
   176	        } else if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   177	            // Otherwise, it is a special file (e.g. device file) and we should
   178	            // return the file-specific mappable object.
   179	            file_io.mappable()
   180	        } else {
   181	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the file is not mappable");
   182	        }
   183	    }
   184	
   185	    fn test_range_lock(&self, lock: RangeLockItem) -> Result<RangeLockItem> {
   186	        let mut req_lock = lock.clone();
   187	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   188	            if let Some(range_lock_list) = extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   189	                req_lock = range_lock_list.test_lock(lock);
   190	            } else {
   191	                // The range lock could be placed if there is no lock list
   192	                req_lock.set_type(RangeLockType::Unlock);
   193	            }
   194	        } else {
   195	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, the lock could be placed");
   196	            // Some file systems may not support range lock like procfs and sysfs
   197	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   198	            req_lock.set_type(RangeLockType::Unlock);
   199	        }
   200	        Ok(req_lock)
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    fn set_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   204	        if RangeLockType::Unlock == lock.type_() {
   205	            self.unlock_range_lock(lock);
   206	            return Ok(());
   207	        }
   208	
   209	        self.check_range_lock_with_access_mode(lock)?;
   210	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   211	            let range_lock_list = match extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   212	                Some(list) => list,
   213	                None => extension.get_or_put_default::<RangeLockList>(),
   214	            };
   215	
   216	            range_lock_list.set_lock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   217	        } else {
   218	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, let the lock could be acquired");
   219	            // Some file systems may not support range lock like procfs and sysfs
   220	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   221	            Ok(())
   222	        }
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    fn release_range_locks(&self) {
   226	        if self.path.inode().extension().is_none() {
   227	            return;
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        let range_lock = RangeLockItem::new(
   231	            RangeLockType::Unlock,
   232	            FileRange::new(0, OFFSET_MAX).unwrap(),
   233	        );
   234	        self.unlock_range_lock(&range_lock);
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    fn unlock_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem) {
   238	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   239	            if let Some(range_lock_list) = extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   240	                range_lock_list.unlock(lock);
   241	            }
   242	        }
   243	    }
   244	
   245	    fn check_range_lock_with_access_mode(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem) -> Result<()> {
   246	        match lock.type_() {
   247	            RangeLockType::ReadLock => {
   248	                if !self.access_mode().is_readable() {
   249	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EBADF, "file not readable");
   250	                }
   251	            }
   252	            RangeLockType::WriteLock => {
   253	                if !self.access_mode().is_writable() {
   254	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EBADF, "file not writable");
   255	                }
   256	            }
   257	            _ => (),
   258	        }
   259	        Ok(())
   260	    }
   261	
   262	    fn set_flock(&self, lock: FlockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   263	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   264	            let flock_list = match extension.get::<FlockList>() {
   265	                Some(list) => list,
   266	                None => extension.get_or_put_default::<FlockList>(),
   267	            };
   268	
   269	            flock_list.set_lock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   270	        } else {
   271	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, let the lock could be acquired");
   272	            // Some file systems may not support flock like procfs and sysfs
   273	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   274	            Ok(())
   275	        }
   276	    }
   277	
   278	    fn unlock_flock<R>(&self, req_owner: &InodeHandle<R>) {
   279	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   280	            if let Some(flock_list) = extension.get::<FlockList>() {
   281	                flock_list.unlock(req_owner);
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	    }
   285	}
   286	
   287	#[inherit_methods(from = "self.path")]
   288	impl InodeHandle_ {
   289	    pub fn size(&self) -> usize;
   290	    pub fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata;
   291	    pub fn mode(&self) -> Result<InodeMode>;
   292	    pub fn set_mode(&self, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<()>;
   293	    pub fn owner(&self) -> Result<Uid>;
   294	    pub fn set_owner(&self, uid: Uid) -> Result<()>;
   295	    pub fn group(&self) -> Result<Gid>;
   296	    pub fn set_group(&self, gid: Gid) -> Result<()>;
   297	}
   298	
   299	impl Debug for InodeHandle_ {
   300	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut core::fmt::Formatter) -> core::fmt::Result {
   301	        f.debug_struct("InodeHandle_")
   302	            .field("path", &self.path)
   303	            .field("offset", &self.offset())
   304	            .field("access_mode", &self.access_mode())
   305	            .field("status_flags", &self.status_flags())
   306	            .finish()
   307	    }
   308	}
   309	
   310	/// Methods for both dyn and static
   311	impl<R> InodeHandle<R> {
   312	    pub fn path(&self) -> &Path {
   313	        &self.0.path
   314	    }
   315	
   316	    pub fn test_range_lock(&self, lock: RangeLockItem) -> Result<RangeLockItem> {
   317	        self.0.test_range_lock(lock)
   318	    }
   319	
   320	    pub fn set_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   321	        self.0.set_range_lock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   322	    }
   323	
   324	    pub fn release_range_locks(&self) {
   325	        self.0.release_range_locks()
   326	    }
   327	
   328	    pub fn set_flock(&self, lock: FlockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   329	        self.0.set_flock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   330	    }
   331	
   332	    pub fn unlock_flock(&self) {
   333	        self.0.unlock_flock(self);
   334	    }
   335	
   336	    pub fn offset(&self) -> usize {
   337	        self.0.offset()
   338	    }
   339	}
   340	
   341	impl<R> Drop for InodeHandle<R> {
   342	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   343	        self.release_range_locks();
   344	        self.unlock_flock();
   345	    }
   346	}
   347	
   348	pub trait FileIo: Pollable + Send + Sync + 'static {
   349	    fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize>;
   350	
   351	    fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize>;
   352	
   353	    /// See [`FileLike::mappable`].
   354	    fn mappable(&self) -> Result<Mappable> {
   355	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the file is not mappable");
   356	    }
   357	
   358	    fn ioctl(&self, cmd: IoctlCmd, arg: usize) -> Result<i32> {
   359	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "ioctl is not supported");
   360	    }
   361	}
   362	
   363	pub fn do_seek_util(inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>, offset: &Mutex<usize>, pos: SeekFrom) -> Result<usize> {
   364	    let mut offset = offset.lock();
   365	    let new_offset: isize = match pos {
   366	        SeekFrom::Start(off /* as usize */) => {
   367	            if off > isize::MAX as usize {
   368	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "file offset is too large");
   369	            }
   370	            off as isize
   371	        }
   372	        SeekFrom::End(off /* as isize */) => {
   373	            let file_size = inode.size() as isize;
   374	            assert!(file_size >= 0);
   375	            file_size
   376	                .checked_add(off)
   377	                .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EOVERFLOW, "file offset overflow"))?
   378	        }
   379	        SeekFrom::Current(off /* as isize */) => (*offset as isize)
   380	            .checked_add(off)
   381	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EOVERFLOW, "file offset overflow"))?,
   382	    };
   383	    if new_offset < 0 {
   384	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "file offset must not be negative");
   385	    }
   386	    // Invariant: 0 <= new_offset <= isize::MAX
   387	    let new_offset = new_offset as usize;
   388	    *offset = new_offset;
   389	    Ok(new_offset)
   390	}
   391	
   392	pub fn do_fallocate_util(
   393	    inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>,
   394	    status_flags: StatusFlags,
   395	    mode: FallocMode,
   396	    offset: usize,
   397	    len: usize,
   398	) -> Result<()> {
   399	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND)
   400	        && (mode == FallocMode::PunchHoleKeepSize
   401	            || mode == FallocMode::CollapseRange
   402	            || mode == FallocMode::InsertRange)
   403	    {
   404	        return_errno_with_message!(
   405	            Errno::EPERM,
   406	            "the flags do not work on the append-only file"
   407	        );
   408	    }
   409	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) || status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_PATH) {
   410	        return_errno_with_message!(
   411	            Errno::EBADF,
   412	            "currently fallocate file with O_DIRECT or O_PATH is not supported"
   413	        );
   414	    }
   415	
   416	    inode.fallocate(mode, offset, len)
   417	}
   418	
   419	pub fn do_resize_util(
   420	    inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>,
   421	    status_flags: StatusFlags,
   422	    new_size: usize,
   423	) -> Result<()> {
   424	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
   425	        // FIXME: It's allowed to `ftruncate` an append-only file on Linux.
   426	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "can not resize append-only file");
   427	    }
   428	    inode.resize(new_size)
   429	}

---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.
---

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> 3	#![expect(unused_variables)]
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-level `#![expect(unused_variables)]` suppresses `unused_variables` for every item in `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs`, so future unused locals or parameters in unrelated code are hidden by this file-wide attribute.

**Fix.** Remove the module-level attribute and either prefix the known intentionally unused parameters with `_` or put `#[expect(unused_variables)]` on only the specific item that needs it.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 203

> ```diff
> 203	    fn set_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_range_lock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, so callers must remember which boolean value selects blocking versus nonblocking behavior.

**Fix.** Replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 262

> ```diff
> 262	    fn set_flock(&self, lock: FlockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_flock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, which makes call sites encode lock behavior as an untyped boolean.

**Fix.** Use the same typed blocking mode recommended for `set_range_lock`, for example `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}`.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> 3	//! Form file paths within and across FSes with dentries and mount points.
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (nit): The module summary starts with the verb phrase `Form file paths...`; module docs should start with a noun phrase, not an imperative/function-style summary.

**Fix.** Rewrite the first `//!` line as a noun phrase, for example `//! File path formation within and across file systems with dentries and mount points.`

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 269

> ```diff
> 269	    pub fn bind_mount_to(&self, dst_path: &Self, recursive: bool, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): The public `bind_mount_to` API takes `recursive: bool`, so call sites such as `bind_mount_to(dst_path, true, ctx)` do not make the selected mount behavior obvious.

**Fix.** Replace `recursive: bool` with a typed mode such as `MountRecursion::{Single, Recursive}` and pass that through to `clone_mount_tree`.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 329

> ```diff
> 325	    /// Sets the propagation type of the mount of this `Path`.
> 326	    pub fn set_mount_propagation(
> 327	        &self,
> 328	        prop: MountPropType,
> 329	        recursive: bool,
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): The public `set_mount_propagation` API takes `recursive: bool`, which makes callers encode propagation scope as a bare `true` or `false` instead of a named domain value.

**Fix.** Use a typed scope parameter such as `MountRecursion::{Single, Recursive}` or `PropagationScope::{Single, Recursive}`.
