// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};

use super::{
    file_handle::FileLike,
    utils::{mkmod, AccessMode, Endpoint, EndpointState, InodeType, Metadata, StatusFlags},
};
use crate::{
    events::IoEvents,
    prelude::*,
    process::{
        signal::{PollHandle, Pollable},
        Gid, Uid,
    },
    time::clocks::RealTimeCoarseClock,
    util::ring_buffer::{RbConsumer, RbProducer, RingBuffer},
};

const DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE: usize = 65536;

/// Maximum number of bytes guaranteed to be written to a pipe atomically.
///
/// If the number of bytes to be written is less than the threshold, the write must be atomic.
/// A non-blocking atomic write may fail with `EAGAIN`, even if there is room for a partial write.
/// In other words, a partial write is not allowed for an atomic write.
///
/// For more details, see the description of `PIPE_BUF` in
/// <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/pipe.7.html>.
#[cfg(not(ktest))]
const PIPE_BUF: usize = 4096;
#[cfg(ktest)]
const PIPE_BUF: usize = 2;

pub fn new_pair() -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    new_pair_with_capacity(DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE)
}

fn new_pair_with_capacity(capacity: usize) -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    let (producer, consumer) = RingBuffer::new(capacity).split();
    let (producer_state, consumer_state) =
        Endpoint::new_pair(EndpointState::default(), EndpointState::default());

    Ok((
        PipeReader::new(consumer, consumer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
        PipeWriter::new(producer, producer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
    ))
}

pub struct PipeReader {
    consumer: Mutex<RbConsumer<u8>>,
    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    status_flags: AtomicU32,
}

impl PipeReader {
    fn new(
        consumer: RbConsumer<u8>,
        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
        status_flags: StatusFlags,
    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;

        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
            consumer: Mutex::new(consumer),
            state,
            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
        }))
    }

    fn try_read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        let read = || {
            let mut consumer = self.consumer.lock();
            consumer.read_fallible(writer)
        };

        self.state.read_with(read)
    }

    fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
        let mut events = IoEvents::empty();
        if self.state.is_peer_shutdown() {
            events |= IoEvents::HUP;
        }
        if !self.consumer.lock().is_empty() {
            events |= IoEvents::IN;
        }
        events
    }
}

impl Pollable for PipeReader {
    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
        self.state
            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.check_io_events())
    }
}

impl FileLike for PipeReader {
    fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        if !writer.has_avail() {
            // Even the peer endpoint (`PipeWriter`) has been closed, reading an empty buffer is
            // still fine.
            return Ok(0);
        }

        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
            self.try_read(writer)
        } else {
            self.wait_events(IoEvents::IN, None, || self.try_read(writer))
        }
    }

    fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
        StatusFlags::from_bits_truncate(self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    }

    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
        check_status_flags(new_flags)?;

        self.status_flags.store(new_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
        AccessMode::O_RDONLY
    }

    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
        // This is a dummy implementation.
        // TODO: Add "PipeFS" and link `PipeReader` to it.
        let now = RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time();
        Metadata {
            dev: 0,
            ino: 0,
            size: 0,
            blk_size: 0,
            blocks: 0,
            atime: now,
            mtime: now,
            ctime: now,
            type_: InodeType::NamedPipe,
            mode: mkmod!(u+r),
            nlinks: 1,
            uid: Uid::new_root(),
            gid: Gid::new_root(),
            rdev: 0,
        }
    }
}

impl Drop for PipeReader {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.state.peer_shutdown();
    }
}

pub struct PipeWriter {
    producer: Mutex<RbProducer<u8>>,
    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    status_flags: AtomicU32,
}

impl PipeWriter {
    fn new(
        producer: RbProducer<u8>,
        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
        status_flags: StatusFlags,
    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;

        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
            producer: Mutex::new(producer),
            state,
            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
        }))
    }

    fn try_write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        let write = || {
            let mut producer = self.producer.lock();
            if reader.remain() <= PIPE_BUF && producer.free_len() < reader.remain() {
                // No sufficient space for an atomic write
                return Ok(0);
            }
            producer.write_fallible(reader)
        };

        self.state.write_with(write)
    }

    fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
        if self.state.is_shutdown() {
            IoEvents::ERR | IoEvents::OUT
        } else if self.producer.lock().free_len() >= PIPE_BUF {
            IoEvents::OUT
        } else {
            IoEvents::empty()
        }
    }
}

impl Pollable for PipeWriter {
    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
        self.state
            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.check_io_events())
    }
}

impl FileLike for PipeWriter {
    fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        if !reader.has_remain() {
            // Even the peer endpoint (`PipeReader`) has been closed, writing an empty buffer is
            // still fine.
            return Ok(0);
        }

        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
            self.try_write(reader)
        } else {
            self.wait_events(IoEvents::OUT, None, || self.try_write(reader))
        }
    }

    fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
        StatusFlags::from_bits_truncate(self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
    }

    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
        check_status_flags(new_flags)?;

        self.status_flags.store(new_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
        Ok(())
    }

    fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
        AccessMode::O_WRONLY
    }

    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
        // This is a dummy implementation.
        // TODO: Add "PipeFS" and link `PipeWriter` to it.
        let now = RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time();
        Metadata {
            dev: 0,
            ino: 0,
            size: 0,
            blk_size: 0,
            blocks: 0,
            atime: now,
            mtime: now,
            ctime: now,
            type_: InodeType::NamedPipe,
            mode: mkmod!(u+w),
            nlinks: 1,
            uid: Uid::new_root(),
            gid: Gid::new_root(),
            rdev: 0,
        }
    }
}

fn check_status_flags(status_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
        // "O_DIRECT .. Older kernels that do not support this flag will indicate this via an
        // EINVAL error."
        //
        // See <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/pipe.2.html>.
        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the `O_DIRECT` flag is not supported");
    }

    // TODO: Setting most of the other flags will succeed on Linux, but their effects need to be
    // validated.

    Ok(())
}

    fn drop(&mut self) {
        self.state.shutdown();

    }
}

impl Pollable for NamedPipe {

impl Inode for RamInode {
    fn page_cache(&self) -> Option<Vmo<Full>> {
        self.inner
            .as_file()
            .map(|page_cache| page_cache.pages().dup())
    }

    fn read_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        let read_len = {
            match &self.inner {
                Inner::File(page_cache) => {
                    let (offset, read_len) = {
                        let file_size = self.size();
                        let start = file_size.min(offset);
                        let end = file_size.min(offset + writer.avail());
                        (start, end - start)
                    };
                    page_cache.pages().read(offset, writer)?;
                    read_len
                }
                Inner::Device(device) => {
                    device.read(writer)?
                    // Typically, devices like "/dev/zero" or "/dev/null" do not require modifying
                    // timestamps here. Please adjust this behavior accordingly if there are special devices.
                }
                Inner::NamedPipe(named_pipe) => named_pipe.read(writer)?,
                _ => return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "read is not supported"),
            }
        };

        if self.typ == InodeType::File {
            self.set_atime(now());
        }
        Ok(read_len)
    }

    fn read_direct_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        self.read_at(offset, writer)
    }

    fn write_at(&self, offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        let written_len = match self.typ {
            InodeType::File => {
                let page_cache = self.inner.as_file().unwrap();

                let file_size = self.size();
                let write_len = reader.remain();
                let new_size = offset + write_len;
                let should_expand_size = new_size > file_size;
                let new_size_aligned = new_size.align_up(BLOCK_SIZE);
                if should_expand_size {
                    page_cache.resize(new_size_aligned)?;
                }
                page_cache.pages().write(offset, reader)?;

                let now = now();
                let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
                inode_meta.set_mtime(now);
                inode_meta.set_ctime(now);
                if should_expand_size {
                    inode_meta.size = new_size;
                    inode_meta.blocks = new_size_aligned / BLOCK_SIZE;
                }
                write_len
            }
            InodeType::CharDevice | InodeType::BlockDevice => {
                let device = self.inner.as_device().unwrap();
                device.write(reader)?
                // Typically, devices like "/dev/zero" or "/dev/null" do not require modifying
                // timestamps here. Please adjust this behavior accordingly if there are special devices.
            }
            InodeType::NamedPipe => {
                let named_pipe = self.inner.as_named_pipe().unwrap();
                named_pipe.write(reader)?
            }
            _ => return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "write is not supported"),
        };
        Ok(written_len)
    }

    fn write_direct_at(&self, offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        self.write_at(offset, reader)
    }

    fn size(&self) -> usize {
        self.metadata.lock().size
    }

    fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
        if self.typ != InodeType::File {
            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
        }

        let file_size = self.size();
            blocks: inode_metadata.blocks,
            atime: inode_metadata.atime,
            mtime: inode_metadata.mtime,
            ctime: inode_metadata.ctime,
            type_: self.typ,
            mode: inode_metadata.mode,
            nlinks: inode_metadata.nlinks,
            uid: inode_metadata.uid,
            gid: inode_metadata.gid,
            rdev,
        }
    }

    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
        if !self.typ.is_device() {
            return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) & mask;
        }

        let device = self
            .inner
            .as_device()
            .expect("[Internal error] self.typ is device, while self.inner is not");
        device.poll(mask, poller)
    }

    fn fs(&self) -> Arc<dyn FileSystem> {
        Weak::upgrade(&self.fs).unwrap()
    }

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 15

> ```diff
> pub struct NamedPipe {
>     reader: Arc<PipeReader>,
>     writer: Arc<PipeWriter>,
> }
> ```

Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.

**Fix.** Do not model FIFO open state as permanent endpoint `Arc`s stored in `NamedPipe`. Keep shared FIFO state in the inode, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380

> ```diff
> match inode_type {
>     InodeType::NamedPipe => {
>         warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
>         debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
>     }
>     InodeType::SymLink => {
> ```

Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args` and initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, or otherwise pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142

> ```diff
> fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
>     if !self.typ.is_device() {
>         return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) & mask;
>     }
> 
>     let device = self
> ```

Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.

**Fix.** Special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()` and delegate to real FIFO readiness checks. As part of that, replace the `NamedPipe::poll()` stub with polling based on the per-open reader/writer endpoint and register the provided `PollHandle` with the underlying pipe `Pollee`.

## Security

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 348

> ```diff
> 347    /// Creates a `Path` by making an inode of the `type_` with the `mode`.
> 348    pub fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
> 349        let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
> 350        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), inner))
> 351    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::mknod()` forwards directly to `self.dentry.mknod()` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_mknodat()` resolves the parent path and then calls `dir_path.mknod(...)`, so a process that only has search permission on a directory can still create a FIFO or device node in it.

**Fix.** Validate parent directory write permission in `Path::mknod()` before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:

```rust
if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE).is_err() {
    return_errno!(Errno::EACCES);
}
```

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 354

> ```diff
> 353    /// Links a new name for the `Path`.
> 354    pub fn link(&self, old: &Self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
> 355        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&old.mount, &self.mount) {
> 356            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
> 357        }
> 358
> 359        self.dentry.link(&old.dentry, name)
> 360    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::link()` does not validate `MAY_WRITE` on the destination directory before adding a new hard link. `sys_linkat()` resolves `new_path` and then calls `new_path.link(...)`, so an unprivileged caller can create directory entries in a non-writable destination directory.

**Fix.** Check `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` in `Path::link()` before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363

> ```diff
> 362    /// Renames a `Path` to the new `Path` by `rename()` the inner inode.
> 363    pub fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, new_dir: &Self, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
> 364        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&self.mount, &new_dir.mount) {
> 365            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
> 366        }
> 367
> 368        self.dentry.rename(old_name, &new_dir.dentry, new_name)
> 369    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rename()` does not validate write permission on either the source parent directory or the destination parent directory. `sys_renameat2()` resolves both directories and calls `old_dir_path.rename(...)`, so a caller with only search permission can remove an entry from `old_dir_path`, create or replace an entry in `new_dir`, or both.

**Fix.** Require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415

> ```diff
> 413    #[inherit_methods(from = "self.dentry")]
> 414    impl Path {
> 415        pub fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
> 416        pub fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::unlink()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` with no parent directory write-permission check. `sys_unlinkat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.unlink(name)`, so a caller can delete non-directory entries from a directory even when `Permission::MAY_WRITE` would deny the operation.

**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 416

> ```diff
> 413    #[inherit_methods(from = "self.dentry")]
> 414    impl Path {
> 415        pub fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
> 416        pub fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()>;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rmdir()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_rmdirat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.rmdir(name)`, so a caller can remove empty subdirectories from a non-writable directory.

**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.

#[repr(u16)]
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq, TryFromInt)]
pub enum InodeType {
    Unknown = 0o000000,
    NamedPipe = 0o010000,
    CharDevice = 0o020000,
    Dir = 0o040000,
    BlockDevice = 0o060000,
    File = 0o100000,
    SymLink = 0o120000,
    Socket = 0o140000,
}

impl InodeType {
    pub fn is_regular_file(&self) -> bool {
        *self == InodeType::File
    }

    pub fn is_directory(&self) -> bool {
        *self == InodeType::Dir
    }

    pub fn is_device(&self) -> bool {
        *self == InodeType::BlockDevice || *self == InodeType::CharDevice
    }

    /// Parse the inode type in the `mode` from syscall, and convert it into `InodeType`.
    pub fn from_raw_mode(mut mode: u16) -> Result<Self> {
        const TYPE_MASK: u16 = 0o170000;
        mode &= TYPE_MASK;

        // Special case
        if mode == 0 {
            return Ok(Self::File);
        }
        Self::try_from(mode & TYPE_MASK)
            .map_err(|_| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid file type"))
    }
}

impl From<DeviceType> for InodeType {
    fn from(type_: DeviceType) -> InodeType {
        match type_ {
            DeviceType::Char => InodeType::CharDevice,
            DeviceType::Block => InodeType::BlockDevice,
            DeviceType::Misc => InodeType::CharDevice,
        }
    }
}

bitflags! {
    pub struct Permission: u16 {
        // This implementation refers the implementation of linux
        // https://elixir.bootlin.com/linux/v6.0.9/source/include/linux/fs.h#L95
        const MAY_EXEC		= 0x0001;
        const MAY_WRITE		= 0x0002;
        const MAY_READ		= 0x0004;
        const MAY_APPEND    = 0x0008;
        const MAY_ACCESS	= 0x0010;
        const MAY_OPEN		= 0x0020;
        const MAY_CHDIR		= 0x0040;
        const MAY_NOT_BLOCK	= 0x0080;
    }
}
impl Permission {
    pub fn may_read(&self) -> bool {
        self.contains(Self::MAY_READ)
    }

    pub fn may_write(&self) -> bool {
        self.contains(Self::MAY_WRITE)
    }

    pub fn may_exec(&self) -> bool {
        self.contains(Self::MAY_EXEC)
    }
}
impl From<AccessMode> for Permission {
    fn from(access_mode: AccessMode) -> Permission {
        match access_mode {
            AccessMode::O_RDONLY => Permission::MAY_READ,
            AccessMode::O_WRONLY => Permission::MAY_WRITE,
            AccessMode::O_RDWR => Permission::MAY_READ | Permission::MAY_WRITE,
        }
    }
}

#![expect(unused_variables)]

//! Opened Inode-backed File Handle

mod dyn_cap;
mod static_cap;

use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};

use aster_rights::Rights;
use inherit_methods_macro::inherit_methods;

use crate::{
    events::IoEvents,
    fs::{
        file_handle::{FileLike, Mappable},
        path::Path,
        utils::{
            AccessMode, DirentVisitor, FallocMode, FileRange, FlockItem, FlockList, Inode,
            InodeMode, InodeType, IoctlCmd, Metadata, RangeLockItem, RangeLockList, RangeLockType,
            SeekFrom, StatusFlags, OFFSET_MAX,
        },
    },
    prelude::*,
    process::{
        signal::{PollHandle, Pollable},
        Gid, Uid,
    },
};

#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct InodeHandle<R = Rights>(Arc<InodeHandle_>, R);

struct InodeHandle_ {
    path: Path,
    /// `file_io` is Similar to `file_private` field in `file` structure in linux. If
    /// `file_io` is Some, typical file operations including `read`, `write`, `poll`,
    /// `ioctl` will be provided by `file_io`, instead of `path`.
    file_io: Option<Arc<dyn FileIo>>,
    offset: Mutex<usize>,
    access_mode: AccessMode,
    status_flags: AtomicU32,
}

impl InodeHandle_ {
    pub fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
            return file_io.read(writer);
        }

        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
            return self.read_at(0, writer);
        }

        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();

        let len = self.read_at(*offset, writer)?;

        *offset += len;
        Ok(len)
    }

    pub fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
            return file_io.write(reader);
        }

        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
            return self.write_at(0, reader);
        }

        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();

        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
            *offset = self.path.size();
        }

        let len = self.write_at(*offset, reader)?;

        *offset += len;
        Ok(len)
    }

    pub fn read_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
            todo!("support read_at for FileIo");
        }

        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
            self.path.inode().read_direct_at(offset, writer)
        } else {
            self.path.inode().read_at(offset, writer)
        }
    }

    pub fn write_at(&self, mut offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
            todo!("support write_at for FileIo");
        }

        let status_flags = self.status_flags();
        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
            // If the file has the O_APPEND flag, the offset is ignored
            offset = self.path.size();
        }

        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
            self.path.inode().write_direct_at(offset, reader)
        } else {
            self.path.inode().write_at(offset, reader)
        }
    }

    pub fn seek(&self, pos: SeekFrom) -> Result<usize> {
        do_seek_util(self.path.inode(), &self.offset, pos)
    }

    pub fn offset(&self) -> usize {
        let offset = self.offset.lock();
        *offset
    }

    pub fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
        do_resize_util(self.path.inode(), self.status_flags(), new_size)
    }

    pub fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
        self.access_mode
    }

    pub fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
        let bits = self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
        StatusFlags::from_bits(bits).unwrap()
    }

    pub fn set_status_flags(&self, new_status_flags: StatusFlags) {
        self.status_flags
            .store(new_status_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
    }

    pub fn readdir(&self, visitor: &mut dyn DirentVisitor) -> Result<usize> {
        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
        let read_cnt = self.path.inode().readdir_at(*offset, visitor)?;
        *offset += read_cnt;
        Ok(read_cnt)
    }

    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {

    /// Lookups a target `Dentry` from the file system.
    pub(super) fn lookup_via_fs(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Arc<Dentry>> {
        let children = self.children.upread();

        let inode = match self.inode.lookup(name) {
            Ok(inode) => inode,
            Err(e) => {
                if e.error() == Errno::ENOENT && self.is_dentry_cacheable() {
                    children.upgrade().insert_negative(String::from(name));
                }
                return Err(e);
            }
        };
        let name = String::from(name);
        let target = Self::new(inode, DentryOptions::Leaf((name.clone(), self.this())));

        if target.is_dentry_cacheable() {
            children.upgrade().insert(name, target.clone());
        }

        Ok(target)
    }

    /// Creates a `Dentry` by making an inode of the `type_` with the `mode`.
    pub(super) fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
            return_errno!(Errno::ENOTDIR);
        }

        let children = self.children.upread();
        if children.contains_valid(name) {
            return_errno!(Errno::EEXIST);
        }

        let inode = self.inode.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
        let name = String::from(name);
        let new_child = Dentry::new(inode, DentryOptions::Leaf((name.clone(), self.this())));

        if new_child.is_dentry_cacheable() {
            children.upgrade().insert(name, new_child.clone());
        }

        Ok(new_child)
    }

    /// Links a new name for the `Dentry` by `link()` the inner inode.
    pub(super) fn link(&self, old: &Arc<Self>, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
            return_errno!(Errno::ENOTDIR);
        }

        let children = self.children.upread();
        if children.contains_valid(name) {
            return_errno!(Errno::EEXIST);
        }

        let old_inode = old.inode();
        self.inode.link(old_inode, name)?;
        let name = String::from(name);
        let dentry = Dentry::new(
            old_inode.clone(),
            DentryOptions::Leaf((name.clone(), self.this())),
        );

        if dentry.is_dentry_cacheable() {
            children.upgrade().insert(name, dentry.clone());
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Deletes a `Dentry` by `unlink()` the inner inode.
    pub(super) fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
            return_errno!(Errno::ENOTDIR);
        }

        let children = self.children.upread();
        children.check_mountpoint(name)?;

        self.inode.unlink(name)?;

        let mut children = children.upgrade();
        children.delete(name);
        Ok(())
    }

 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 25
 
@@ -150,7 +150,7 @@

-**Fix.** Rename the binding to `try_visit_fn`, or extract it into a private helper if keeping the traversal logic outside `visit_entry` would read better.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `closure-fn-suffix` comments: rename the binding to `try_visit_fn`, or extract it into a private helper if keeping the traversal logic outside `visit_entry` would read better.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 945
 
@@ -162,7 +162,7 @@

-**Fix.** Rename it to `check_replace_inode_fn`, or make it a private helper function since it does not need to capture local state.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `closure-fn-suffix` comments: rename it to `check_replace_inode_fn`, or make it a private helper function since it does not need to capture local state.
 
 ## Correctness
 
@@ -177,7 +177,7 @@
 
 Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.
 
-**Fix.** Do not model FIFO open state as permanent endpoint `Arc`s stored in `NamedPipe`. Keep shared FIFO state in the inode, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: stop storing permanent endpoint `Arc`s on the inode. Keep shared FIFO state separately, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380
 
@@ -192,7 +192,7 @@
 
 Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.
 
-**Fix.** Create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args` and initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, or otherwise pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args`, initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, and pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142
 
@@ -207,7 +207,7 @@
 
 Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.
 
-**Fix.** Special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()` and delegate to real FIFO readiness checks. As part of that, replace the `NamedPipe::poll()` stub with polling based on the per-open reader/writer endpoint and register the provided `PollHandle` with the underlying pipe `Pollee`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()`, delegate to real FIFO readiness checks, and make `NamedPipe::poll()` forward to the per-open reader/writer endpoint with the provided `PollHandle`.

 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::link()` does not validate `MAY_WRITE` on the destination directory before adding a new hard link. `sys_linkat()` resolves `new_path` and then calls `new_path.link(...)`, so an unprivileged caller can create directory entries in a non-writable destination directory.

 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363
 
@@ -263,7 +263,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rename()` does not validate write permission on either the source parent directory or the destination parent directory. `sys_renameat2()` resolves both directories and calls `old_dir_path.rename(...)`, so a caller with only search permission can remove an entry from `old_dir_path`, create or replace an entry in `new_dir`, or both.
 
-**Fix.** Require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.

 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415

 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::unlink()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` with no parent directory write-permission check. `sys_unlinkat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.unlink(name)`, so a caller can delete non-directory entries from a directory even when `Permission::MAY_WRITE` would deny the operation.

 ## Correctness
 
@@ -177,7 +177,7 @@
 
 Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.
 
-**Fix.** Do not model FIFO open state as permanent endpoint `Arc`s stored in `NamedPipe`. Keep shared FIFO state in the inode, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: stop storing permanent endpoint `Arc`s on the inode. Keep shared FIFO state separately, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380
 
@@ -192,7 +192,7 @@
 
 Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.
 
-**Fix.** Create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args` and initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, or otherwise pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args`, initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, and pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142
 
@@ -207,7 +207,7 @@
 
 Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.
 
-**Fix.** Special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()` and delegate to real FIFO readiness checks. As part of that, replace the `NamedPipe::poll()` stub with polling based on the per-open reader/writer endpoint and register the provided `PollHandle` with the underlying pipe `Pollee`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()`, delegate to real FIFO readiness checks, and make `NamedPipe::poll()` forward to the per-open reader/writer endpoint with the provided `PollHandle`.
 
 ## Security
 
@@ -223,7 +223,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::mknod()` forwards directly to `self.dentry.mknod()` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_mknodat()` resolves the parent path and then calls `dir_path.mknod(...)`, so a process that only has search permission on a directory can still create a FIFO or device node in it.
 
-**Fix.** Validate parent directory write permission in `Path::mknod()` before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: add a parent-directory write-permission helper on `Path` and use it before forwarding to `dentry.mknod`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
 
 ```rust

@@ -246,7 +246,7 @@

 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363
 
@@ -263,7 +263,7 @@

-**Fix.** Require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.

 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The main risk here is the new FIFO plumbing: `NamedPipe` is wired into ramfs, but endpoint lifetime, `O_NONBLOCK` propagation, and readiness reporting are still inconsistent, so FIFO `open`/`read`/`write`/`poll` behavior will not match user expectations yet. The `Path`-level mutators exposed through syscalls also need explicit parent write checks before they can be treated as safe boundary APIs. The rest of the findings are mostly maintainability cleanup around API shape and local naming.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -30,7 +30,7 @@
 
 `no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_range_lock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, so callers must remember which boolean value selects blocking versus nonblocking behavior.
 
-**Fix.** Replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.

+**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: use the same typed blocking mode recommended for `set_range_lock`, for example `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}`.

 ## Correctness
 
@@ -177,7 +177,7 @@
 
 Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.
 
-**Fix.** Do not model FIFO open state as permanent endpoint `Arc`s stored in `NamedPipe`. Keep shared FIFO state in the inode, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: stop storing permanent endpoint `Arc`s on the inode. Keep shared FIFO state separately, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380
 
@@ -192,7 +192,7 @@
 
 Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.
 
-**Fix.** Create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args` and initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, or otherwise pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args`, initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, and pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142
 
@@ -207,7 +207,7 @@
 
 Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.
 
-**Fix.** Special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()` and delegate to real FIFO readiness checks. As part of that, replace the `NamedPipe::poll()` stub with polling based on the per-open reader/writer endpoint and register the provided `PollHandle` with the underlying pipe `Pollee`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()`, delegate to real FIFO readiness checks, and make `NamedPipe::poll()` forward to the per-open reader/writer endpoint with the provided `PollHandle`.
 
 ## Security
 
@@ -223,7 +223,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::mknod()` forwards directly to `self.dentry.mknod()` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_mknodat()` resolves the parent path and then calls `dir_path.mknod(...)`, so a process that only has search permission on a directory can still create a FIFO or device node in it.
 
-**Fix.** Validate parent directory write permission in `Path::mknod()` before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: add a parent-directory write-permission helper on `Path` and use it before forwarding to `dentry.mknod`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
 
 ```rust
 if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE).is_err() {
@@ -246,7 +246,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::link()` does not validate `MAY_WRITE` on the destination directory before adding a new hard link. `sys_linkat()` resolves `new_path` and then calls `new_path.link(...)`, so an unprivileged caller can create directory entries in a non-writable destination directory.
 
-**Fix.** Check `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` in `Path::link()` before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: use the same `Path` write-permission helper before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363
 
@@ -263,7 +263,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rename()` does not validate write permission on either the source parent directory or the destination parent directory. `sys_renameat2()` resolves both directories and calls `old_dir_path.rename(...)`, so a caller with only search permission can remove an entry from `old_dir_path`, create or replace an entry in `new_dir`, or both.
 
-**Fix.** Require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415
 
@@ -276,7 +276,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::unlink()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` with no parent directory write-permission check. `sys_unlinkat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.unlink(name)`, so a caller can delete non-directory entries from a directory even when `Permission::MAY_WRITE` would deny the operation.
 
-**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 416
 
@@ -289,4 +289,4 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rmdir()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_rmdirat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.rmdir(name)`, so a caller can remove empty subdirectories from a non-writable directory.
 
-**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.

 # Summary
 
-<!-- SUMMARY -->
+The main risk here is the new FIFO plumbing: `NamedPipe` is wired into ramfs, but endpoint lifetime, `O_NONBLOCK` propagation, and readiness reporting are still inconsistent, so FIFO `open`/`read`/`write`/`poll` behavior will not match user expectations yet. The `Path`-level mutators exposed through syscalls also need explicit parent write checks before they can be treated as safe boundary APIs. The rest of the findings are mostly maintainability cleanup around API shape and local naming.
 
 ## Maintainability
 
@@ -30,7 +30,7 @@
 
 `no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_range_lock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, so callers must remember which boolean value selects blocking versus nonblocking behavior.
 
-**Fix.** Replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 262
 
@@ -40,7 +40,7 @@
 
 `no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_flock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, which makes call sites encode lock behavior as an untyped boolean.
 
-**Fix.** Use the same typed blocking mode recommended for `set_range_lock`, for example `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: use the same typed blocking mode recommended for `set_range_lock`, for example `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 3

+**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: replace `recursive: bool` with a typed mode such as `MountRecursion::{Single, Recursive}` and pass that through to `clone_mount_tree`.

+**Fix.** Shared with the other `closure-fn-suffix` comments: rename the binding to `read_fn` and pass `read_fn` to `self.state.read_with`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs` line 180

 `closure-fn-suffix` (nit): The `write` binding stores a closure but lacks the `_fn` suffix, so `self.state.write_with(write)` does not signal that `write` is callable.
 
-**Fix.** Rename the binding to `write_fn` and pass `write_fn` to `self.state.write_with`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `closure-fn-suffix` comments: rename the binding to `write_fn` and pass `write_fn` to `self.state.write_with`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs` line 295

-**Fix.** Rename the closure parameters to `write_fn` and `read_fn`, and update their call sites.

 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 945

-**Fix.** Rename it to `check_replace_inode_fn`, or make it a private helper function since it does not need to capture local state.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `closure-fn-suffix` comments: rename it to `check_replace_inode_fn`, or make it a private helper function since it does not need to capture local state.
 
 ## Correctness
 
@@ -177,7 +177,7 @@
 
 Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.
 
-**Fix.** Do not model FIFO open state as permanent endpoint `Arc`s stored in `NamedPipe`. Keep shared FIFO state in the inode, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: stop storing permanent endpoint `Arc`s on the inode. Keep shared FIFO state separately, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380
 
@@ -192,7 +192,7 @@
 
 Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.
 
-**Fix.** Create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args` and initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, or otherwise pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args`, initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, and pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142
 
@@ -207,7 +207,7 @@
 
 Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.
 
-**Fix.** Special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()` and delegate to real FIFO readiness checks. As part of that, replace the `NamedPipe::poll()` stub with polling based on the per-open reader/writer endpoint and register the provided `PollHandle` with the underlying pipe `Pollee`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()`, delegate to real FIFO readiness checks, and make `NamedPipe::poll()` forward to the per-open reader/writer endpoint with the provided `PollHandle`.
 
 ## Security
 
@@ -223,7 +223,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::mknod()` forwards directly to `self.dentry.mknod()` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_mknodat()` resolves the parent path and then calls `dir_path.mknod(...)`, so a process that only has search permission on a directory can still create a FIFO or device node in it.
 
-**Fix.** Validate parent directory write permission in `Path::mknod()` before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: add a parent-directory write-permission helper on `Path` and use it before forwarding to `dentry.mknod`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:
 
 ```rust
 if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE).is_err() {
@@ -246,7 +246,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::link()` does not validate `MAY_WRITE` on the destination directory before adding a new hard link. `sys_linkat()` resolves `new_path` and then calls `new_path.link(...)`, so an unprivileged caller can create directory entries in a non-writable destination directory.
 
-**Fix.** Check `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` in `Path::link()` before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: use the same `Path` write-permission helper before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363
 
@@ -263,7 +263,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rename()` does not validate write permission on either the source parent directory or the destination parent directory. `sys_renameat2()` resolves both directories and calls `old_dir_path.rename(...)`, so a caller with only search permission can remove an entry from `old_dir_path`, create or replace an entry in `new_dir`, or both.
 
-**Fix.** Require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415
 
@@ -276,7 +276,7 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::unlink()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` with no parent directory write-permission check. `sys_unlinkat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.unlink(name)`, so a caller can delete non-directory entries from a directory even when `Permission::MAY_WRITE` would deny the operation.
 
-**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.
 
 ### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 416
 
@@ -289,4 +289,4 @@
 
 `validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rmdir()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_rmdirat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.rmdir(name)`, so a caller can remove empty subdirectories from a non-writable directory.
 
-**Fix.** Replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks `self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)` before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.
+**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.

===== EXPECTED DEFECTS =====
# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs lines 14-45 (persona: development, grounding: FIFO open must block until the peer endpoint is present, severity: major)
   defect: NamedPipe stores one PipeReader and one PipeWriter from creation time and exposes itself as O_RDWR. Opening the FIFO through Path::open only builds a normal InodeHandle; there is no per-open reader/writer handle and no wait in open. Therefore blocking open(O_RDONLY) can return even when no writer has opened the FIFO, and blocking open(O_WRONLY) can return even when no reader has opened it.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must say that FIFO open semantics are wrong because open returns before both read and write ends are present in blocking mode; it should request blocking in open based on access mode and peer presence.

2. location: kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs lines 552-600 (persona: development, grounding: named-pipe read/write ignore the fd's O_NONBLOCK flag, severity: major)
   defect: RamFS dispatches NamedPipe read/write directly to the shared NamedPipe object. NamedPipe::read and NamedPipe::write take no StatusFlags, and InodeHandle::read/write do not pass their per-fd status_flags into FileLike/FileIo. As a result, read/write behavior is determined by the shared pipe endpoints, not by the file descriptor that issued the operation, so O_NONBLOCK on a FIFO fd is not reliably honored.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must identify that named-pipe read/write need access to the caller fd's status_flags; otherwise O_NONBLOCK cannot control whether the operation returns EAGAIN or blocks.

3. location: kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs lines 50-123,158-234 (persona: development, grounding: status_flags must be per file descriptor, not shared by the pipe object, severity: major)
   defect: PipeReader and PipeWriter each store status_flags inside the shared pipe endpoint object. A FIFO inode has one shared NamedPipe with shared reader and writer endpoints, while each open fd has its own InodeHandle status_flags. If two descriptors open the same FIFO with different flags, such as one blocking reader and one nonblocking reader, storing the flag on the shared endpoint cannot represent both descriptors correctly.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must state that status_flags for a named pipe must be per-fd; a shared NamedPipe/PipeReader/PipeWriter flag makes descriptors interfere with each other and cannot support simultaneous blocking and nonblocking opens.

# Summary

The main risk here is the new FIFO plumbing: `NamedPipe` is wired into ramfs, but endpoint lifetime, `O_NONBLOCK` propagation, and readiness reporting are still inconsistent, so FIFO `open`/`read`/`write`/`poll` behavior will not match user expectations yet. The `Path`-level mutators exposed through syscalls also need explicit parent write checks before they can be treated as safe boundary APIs. The rest of the findings are mostly maintainability cleanup around API shape and local naming.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> 3	#![expect(unused_variables)]
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-level `#![expect(unused_variables)]` suppresses `unused_variables` for every item in `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs`, so future unused locals or parameters in unrelated code are hidden by this file-wide attribute.

**Fix.** Remove the module-level attribute and either prefix the known intentionally unused parameters with `_` or put `#[expect(unused_variables)]` on only the specific item that needs it.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 203

> ```diff
> 203	    fn set_range_lock({prompt}self, lock: {prompt}RangeLockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
> ```

`no-bool-args` (minor): The `set_range_lock` API takes `is_nonblocking: bool`, so callers must remember which boolean value selects blocking versus nonblocking behavior.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: replace `is_nonblocking: bool` with a typed mode such as `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}` and pass that typed value through to `RangeLockList::set_lock`.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 262

**Fix.** Shared with the other `no-bool-args` comments: use the same typed blocking mode recommended for `set_range_lock`, for example `BlockingMode::{Blocking, Nonblocking}`.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 15

> ```diff
> pub struct NamedPipe {
>     reader: Arc<PipeReader>,
>     writer: Arc<PipeWriter>,
> }
> ```

Incorrect endpoint lifetime (major): `NamedPipe` keeps permanent `Arc<PipeReader>` and `Arc<PipeWriter>` references in the inode, so closing FIFO file descriptors never drops the actual pipe endpoints. A concrete failure is: create a FIFO, open a writer, close the last reader, then call `write()`; `PipeReader::drop()` never ran because the inode still owns `reader`, so `PipeWriter::write()` does not see peer shutdown and writes or blocks instead of returning `EPIPE`. The symmetric empty-read-after-last-writer case blocks or returns `EAGAIN` instead of EOF for the same reason.

**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: stop storing permanent endpoint `Arc`s on the inode. Keep shared FIFO state separately, create per-open file objects for `O_RDONLY`, `O_WRONLY`, and `O_RDWR`, and have their `Drop` update reader/writer counts and signal the peer only when the last opposite endpoint closes.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380

> ```diff
> match inode_type {
>     InodeType::NamedPipe => {
>         warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
>         debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
>     }
>     InodeType::SymLink => {
> ```

Ignored nonblocking flag (major): `Path::open()` discards `open_args` for `InodeType::NamedPipe`, so `O_NONBLOCK` is stored only on `InodeHandle_` and never reaches the underlying `PipeReader` or `PipeWriter`. A concrete failure is `open("fifo", O_RDONLY | O_NONBLOCK)` followed by `read()` when no data is buffered: `InodeHandle_::read()` goes through `RamInode::read_at()` to `NamedPipe::read()`, whose embedded `PipeReader` still has `StatusFlags::empty()`, so the call waits instead of returning `EAGAIN`.

**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: create a per-open FIFO `FileIo` object from `open_args`, initialize its pipe endpoint with `open_args.status_flags`, and pass the handle's `StatusFlags` into named-pipe `read()` and `write()` before they decide whether to block.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142

> ```diff
> fn poll({prompt}self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<{prompt}mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
>     if !self.typ.is_device() {
>         return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) {prompt} mask;
>     }
> 
>     let device = self
> ```

Incorrect readiness reporting (major): `RamInode::poll()` treats every non-device inode as immediately `IN | OUT`, which includes `InodeType::NamedPipe`. A concrete failure is polling a FIFO read end before any data is written: `poll()` reports `IN`, then a blocking `read()` can still sleep because the pipe buffer is empty. This breaks `poll`/`epoll` users and also bypasses the `NamedPipe::poll()` implementation entirely.

**Fix.** Shared with the other FIFO comments: special-case `Inner::NamedPipe` in `RamInode::poll()`, delegate to real FIFO readiness checks, and make `NamedPipe::poll()` forward to the per-open reader/writer endpoint with the provided `PollHandle`.

## Security

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 348

> ```diff
> 347    /// Creates a `Path` by making an inode of the `type_` with the `mode`.
> 348    pub fn mknod({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
> 349        let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
> 350        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), inner))
> 351    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::mknod()` forwards directly to `self.dentry.mknod()` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_mknodat()` resolves the parent path and then calls `dir_path.mknod(...)`, so a process that only has search permission on a directory can still create a FIFO or device node in it.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: add a parent-directory write-permission helper on `Path` and use it before forwarding to `dentry.mknod`, matching the boundary check already done by `Path::new_fs_child()`:

```rust
if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE).is_err() {
    return_errno!(Errno::EACCES);
}
```

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 354

> ```diff
> 353    /// Links a new name for the `Path`.
> 354    pub fn link({prompt}self, old: {prompt}Self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
> 355        if !Arc::ptr_eq({prompt}old.mount, {prompt}self.mount) {
> 356            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
> 357        }
> 358
> 359        self.dentry.link({prompt}old.dentry, name)
> 360    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::link()` does not validate `MAY_WRITE` on the destination directory before adding a new hard link. `sys_linkat()` resolves `new_path` and then calls `new_path.link(...)`, so an unprivileged caller can create directory entries in a non-writable destination directory.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: use the same `Path` write-permission helper before forwarding to `self.dentry.link(...)`, and return `EACCES` on failure.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 363

> ```diff
> 362    /// Renames a `Path` to the new `Path` by `rename()` the inner inode.
> 363    pub fn rename({prompt}self, old_name: {prompt}str, new_dir: {prompt}Self, new_name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
> 364        if !Arc::ptr_eq({prompt}self.mount, {prompt}new_dir.mount) {
> 365            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
> 366        }
> 367
> 368        self.dentry.rename(old_name, {prompt}new_dir.dentry, new_name)
> 369    }
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rename()` does not validate write permission on either the source parent directory or the destination parent directory. `sys_renameat2()` resolves both directories and calls `old_dir_path.rename(...)`, so a caller with only search permission can remove an entry from `old_dir_path`, create or replace an entry in `new_dir`, or both.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: require `MAY_WRITE` on `self.inode()` and `new_dir.inode()` before calling `self.dentry.rename(...)`; for same-directory renames, one check is sufficient but checking both paths is harmless if they are the same object.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 415

> ```diff
> 413    #[inherit_methods(from = "self.dentry")]
> 414    impl Path {
> 415        pub fn unlink({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()>;
> 416        pub fn rmdir({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()>;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::unlink()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` with no parent directory write-permission check. `sys_unlinkat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.unlink(name)`, so a caller can delete non-directory entries from a directory even when `Permission::MAY_WRITE` would deny the operation.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::unlink()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.unlink(name)`.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 416

> ```diff
> 413    #[inherit_methods(from = "self.dentry")]
> 414    impl Path {
> 415        pub fn unlink({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()>;
> 416        pub fn rmdir({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()>;
> ```

`validate-at-boundaries` (critical): `Path::rmdir()` is inherited directly from `self.dentry` without validating `MAY_WRITE` on the parent directory. `sys_rmdirat()` resolves the parent and calls `dir_path.rmdir(name)`, so a caller can remove empty subdirectories from a non-writable directory.

**Fix.** Shared with the other `validate-at-boundaries` comments: replace the inherited `Path::rmdir()` with an explicit wrapper that checks the parent directory's `MAY_WRITE` permission before forwarding to `self.dentry.rmdir(name)`.
